\displaystyle\begin{cases}\begin{matrix}\scalebox{0.1}{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateMWire.pdf}}\put(0.40119,0.42637){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[335.53992pt]\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.60885){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip 65.0pt-\heightof{\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{}_{{\mathrm{}}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}}/2\hskip 72.0pt\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{}_{{\mathrm{}}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.63959){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\smash{\begin{tabular}[t]{l}\end{tabular}}}}\put(0.77219,0.10885){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip-17.07182pt\par\noindent\hskip 8.5359pt\scalebox{5}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathrm{}}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\end{picture}}\end{matrix}\penalty\ &\text{ with prob. }1-y_{{\mathrm{}\dagger}{\mathrm{}}}x,\\
\begin{matrix}\scalebox{0.1}{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateM.pdf}}\put(0.40119,0.42637){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[335.53992pt]\raggedright\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.60885){\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip 65.0pt-\heightof{\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{Z}_{{\mathrm{}}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}}/2\hskip 72.0pt\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{Z}_{{\mathrm{}}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.63959){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\smash{\begin{tabular}[t]{l}\end{tabular}}}}\put(0.77218,0.10885){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip-17.07182pt\par\noindent\hskip 8.5359pt\scalebox{5}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathrm{}}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateM.pdf}}\end{picture}}\end{matrix}\penalty\ &\text{ with prob. }y_{{\mathrm{}\dagger}{\mathrm{}}}x.\end{cases}

(B.261)

In this case the amplified gate noise amplitudes are (see Eqs (
B.200
) and (
B.206
)):

\displaystyle x_{i}=

\displaystyle\frac{1-\left(1-2\left(1+y_{{\mathrm{}\dagger}{\mathrm{}}}\right)x+4y_{{\mathrm{}\dagger}{\mathrm{}}}x^{2}\right)^{i}\left(1-2x\right)}{2}=\left(1+i\left(1+y_{{\mathrm{}\dagger}{\mathrm{}}}\right)\right)x+\mathcal{O}_{0}\left(x^{2}\right).

(B.262)

This means the coefficients are given by (Eq. (
B.233
)):

\displaystyle c_{\mathrm{Gl};i}=\prod_{m=0:m\neq i}^{N_{\mathrm{M}}}\frac{x_{m}}{x_{m}-x_{i}}=\left(-1\right)^{i}\frac{\prod_{m=0}^{N_{\mathrm{M}}}\left(1+m\left(1+y_{{\mathrm{}\dagger}{\mathrm{}}}\right)\right)}{\left(1+y_{{\mathrm{}\dagger}{\mathrm{}}}\right)^{N_{\mathrm{M}}}\left(1+i\left(1+y_{{\mathrm{}\dagger}{\mathrm{}}}\right)\right)i!\left(N_{\mathrm{M}}-i\right)!}+\mathcal{O}_{0}\left(x\right).

(B.263)

This means the sampling cost is given by (Eq. (
B.246
)):

\displaystyle C_{{\mathrm{Gl}}{\mathrm{}}}=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{N_{\mathrm{M}}}\left|c_{{\mathrm{Gl}};{\mathrm{}j}}\right|=

\displaystyle\frac{\prod_{m=0}^{N_{\mathrm{C}}}\left(1+m\left(1+y_{{\mathrm{}\dagger}{\mathrm{}}}\right)\right){}_{2}F_{1}\left(\frac{1}{1+y_{{\mathrm{}\dagger}{\mathrm{}}}},-N_{\mathrm{M}};\frac{y_{{\mathrm{}\dagger}{\mathrm{}}}+2}{y_{{\mathrm{}\dagger}{\mathrm{}}}+1};-1\right)}{\left(1+y_{{\mathrm{}\dagger}{\mathrm{}}}\right)^{N_{\mathrm{M}}}\left(N_{\mathrm{M}}!\right)}+\mathcal{O}_{0}\left(x\right),

(B.264)

and the length scale factor by (Eq. (
B.249
)):

\displaystyle F_{{\mathrm{Gl}}{\mathrm{}}}=

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{i=0}^{N_{\mathrm{M}}}\left|c_{{\mathrm{Gl}};{\mathrm{}i}}\right|\left(2i+1\right)}{C_{{\mathrm{Gl}}{\mathrm{}}}}=1+2N_{\mathrm{M}}\frac{{}_{2}F_{1}\left(\frac{2+y_{{\mathrm{}\dagger}{\mathrm{}}}}{1+y_{{\mathrm{}\dagger}{\mathrm{}}}},1-N_{\mathrm{M}};\frac{2y_{{\mathrm{}\dagger}{\mathrm{}}}+3}{y_{{\mathrm{}\dagger}{\mathrm{}}}+1};-1\right)}{\left(y_{{\mathrm{}\dagger}{\mathrm{}}}+2\right){}_{2}F_{1}\left(\frac{1}{1+y_{{\mathrm{}\dagger}{\mathrm{}}}},-N_{\mathrm{M}};\frac{y_{{\mathrm{}\dagger}{\mathrm{}}}+2}{y_{{\mathrm{}\dagger}{\mathrm{}}}+1};-1\right)}+\mathcal{O}_{0}\left(x\right).

(B.265)

The proxy bias is given by (see Eqs (
B.5.3
) and (
B.262
)):

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle\heartsuit}};{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle M}}}=

\displaystyle 2^{N_{\mathrm{M}}+1}\left(\prod_{n=0}^{N_{\mathrm{M}}}x_{n}\right)\frac{N_{\mathrm{G}}!}{\left(N_{\mathrm{G}}-N_{\mathrm{M}}-1\right)!\left(N_{\mathrm{M}}+1\right)!}+\mathcal{O}_{0}\left(x^{N_{\mathrm{M}}+2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\prod_{n=0}^{N_{\mathrm{M}}}\left(2N_{\mathrm{G}}x_{n}\right)}{\left(N_{\mathrm{M}}+1\right)!}+\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{LS}}\left(N_{\mathrm{G}}^{N_{\mathrm{M}}}x^{N_{\mathrm{M}}+1},N_{\mathrm{G}}^{N_{\mathrm{M}}+2}x^{N_{\mathrm{M}}+2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(2N_{\mathrm{G}}x\right)^{N_{\mathrm{M}}+1}\frac{\prod_{n=0}^{N_{\mathrm{M}}}\left(\left(1+n\left(1+y_{{\mathrm{}\dagger}{\mathrm{}}}\right)\right)\right)}{\left(N_{\mathrm{M}}+1\right)!}+\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{LS}}\left(N_{\mathrm{G}}^{N_{\mathrm{M}}}x^{N_{\mathrm{M}}+1},N_{\mathrm{G}}^{N_{\mathrm{M}}+2}x^{N_{\mathrm{M}}+2}\right).

(B.266)

So the mitigated noise level is given by:

\displaystyle e_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{G};\mathrm{M}}=e_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{G}}^{N_{\mathrm{M}}+1}\frac{\prod_{n=0}^{N_{\mathrm{M}}}\left(\left(1+n\left(1+y_{{\mathrm{}\dagger}{\mathrm{}}}\right)\right)\right)}{\left(N_{\mathrm{M}}+1\right)!}.

(B.267)

Thus we see that the imperfection in the inverse noisy gate’s noise channel will change the sampling cost and length scale factor, and therefore the runtime scaling; and also the proxy bias and therefore the mitigated noise level. This means that IISM:NA is not robust but it is quasi-robust. Since, though the metrics depend on the value of
y_{{\mathrm{}\dagger}{\mathrm{}}}
to leading order the order of the leading order term is independent of
y_{{\mathrm{}\dagger}{\mathrm{}}}
, i.e. it is still zeroth order in the noise amplitude (
x
) for the sampling cost and the length scale factor and
N_{\mathrm{M}}+1
th order in the noise level (
e_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{G}}
) for the proxy bias. Here we have assumed that, despite the imperfect control, we are able to achieve perfect characterisation. So, we cannot generate noisy gate variants precisely but we know the impact of the errors that we make during the generation procedure.

B.5.7
Qualitative Certification

Now we have all the information we need (see Eqs (
B.239
), (
B.257
), and (
B.259
)) to provide a qualitative evaluation of IISM:NA. We will assess each of the certification criteria for mitigating dephasing noise with IISM:NA. We include the proxy bias (
\varepsilon_{\scriptscriptstyle\heartsuit;\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{M}}
, Eqs (
B.238
) and (
B.240
)), runtime scaling (
S_{{\mathrm{Gl}}{\mathrm{}}}
, Eqs (
B.257
), (
B.257
), (
B.249
), (
B.246
), (
B.233
), and (
B.224
)), and noise boundary (
e_{{\mathrm{B}}{\mathrm{}}}
, Eq. (
B.259
)) again for illustrative purposes.

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\scriptscriptstyle\heartsuit;\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{M}}<

\displaystyle\frac{\left(2e_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{G}}\right)^{N_{\mathrm{M}}+1}}{2}+\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{LS}}\left(\frac{e_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{G}}^{N_{\mathrm{M}}+1}}{N_{\mathrm{G}}},e_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{G}}^{N_{\mathrm{M}}+2}\right),

(B.268)

\displaystyle S_{{\mathrm{Gl}}{\mathrm{}}}\approx

\displaystyle C_{{\mathrm{Gl}}{\mathrm{}}}^{2}F_{{\mathrm{Gl,L}}{\mathrm{}}}=\frac{4^{N_{\mathrm{M}}+1}}{\uppi}\left(1+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{N_{\mathrm{M}}}\right)\right)+\mathcal{O}_{0}\left(x\right),\penalty\ \penalty\ F_{{\mathrm{Gl,L}}{\mathrm{}}}=\frac{\sum_{i=0}^{N_{\mathrm{M}}}\left|c_{{\mathrm{Gl}};{\mathrm{}i}}\right|\left(2i+1\right)}{C_{{\mathrm{Gl}}{\mathrm{}}}},

\displaystyle C_{{\mathrm{Gl}}{\mathrm{}}}=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{N_{\mathrm{M}}}\left|c_{{\mathrm{Gl}};{\mathrm{}i}}\right|,\penalty\ \penalty\ c_{\mathrm{Gl};i}=\prod_{m=0:m\neq i}^{N_{\mathrm{M}}}\frac{\frac{1}{2}\left(1-\left(1-2x\right)^{2m+1}\right)}{\frac{1}{2}\left(1-\left(1-2x\right)^{2m+1}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\left(1-\left(1-2x\right)^{2i+1}\right)},

(B.269)

\displaystyle e_{{\mathrm{B}}{\mathrm{}}}=2^{2\frac{\ln\left(2\varepsilon_{\scriptscriptstyle\pluscirc}\right)}{\ln\left(\uppi S_{\pluscirc}\right)}-1}=\frac{\left(2\varepsilon_{\scriptscriptstyle\pluscirc}\right)^{\frac{\ln\left(4\right)}{\ln\left(\uppi S_{\pluscirc}\right)}}}{2}.

(B.270)

•

Scalable
: Yes (see Eq. (
B.5.7
)); the coefficients of the mitigation are independent of the number of gates in the circuit (see Eq. (
B.233
)), which means so is the sampling cost (Eq. (
B.246
)). The length scale factor is also independent of the number of gates in the circuit as it depends only on the coefficients, the sampling cost, and the number of identity insertions (Eq. (
B.249
)). Since the runtime scaling depends only on the length scale factor and the sampling cost, it is independent of the number of gates in the circuit and therefore the mitigation method is scalable.

•

Unbounded
: No (see Eq. (
B.270
)); the maximum noise level we can mitigate is 0.5, no matter how large we allow the runtime scaling to be so we cannot mitigate arbitrarily large noise levels.

•

Precise
: Yes (see Eq. (
B.268
)); we can make the proxy bias (and therefore the bias) as small as we like by increasing
N_{\mathrm{M}}
(the proxy bias is exponentially suppressed), provided that
e_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{G}}<0.5
. So we can achieve arbitrary accuracy with this method if we are willing to pay the price (of a long runtime).

•

Efficient
: No (see Eq. (
B.5.7
)); even if the noise level (and therefore the noise amplitude,
x
) tends to zero the runtime scaling will not be unity. Even a first order (
N_{\mathrm{M}}=1
) mitigation has
S_{{\mathrm{Gl}}{\mathrm{}}}=6
in the zero-noise limit (
x\rightarrow 0
) and all higher order mitigations would have a larger runtime scaling. So this method does not make good use of reductions in the noise level before mitigation.

•

Robust
: Yes (well quasi-robust, see App.
B.5.6
); the runtime scaling and the proxy bias are modified to leading order if the inverse noisy gate has a different noise amplitude to the original noisy gate but the order of the leading order term is unchanged. This would not be an issue for Hermitian gates as the inverse noisy gate is the same as the original noisy gate.

So IISM:NA is not SUPER but it is SPqR.

Appendix C
Mitigation Methods

Here we will provide the parameters and usage directions for all the mitigation methods studied in this work. We will not provide the derivations of these results here but the full derivations can be found in
[
1
]
and a worked example of the derivation is given in App.
B
for IISM:NA mitigating dephasing noise.

C.1
Noise Models

We consider three types of noise:

•

Stochastic noise:

(C.271)

where
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{}n}{\mathrm{}}}
is the error channel of the
n
th gate,
\mathcal{N}_{{\mathrm{}n}{\mathrm{}}}
is a CPTP channel, and
p
is the noise amplitude. The noise level is given by:

\displaystyle e_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{G}}=e_{{\mathrm{SN}}{\mathrm{}}}=2pN_{\mathrm{G}},

(C.272)

where
N_{\mathrm{G}}
is the number of gates in the circuit.
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{}n}{\mathrm{}}}
is assumed to be closed so:

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{{\mathrm{}n}{\mathrm{}}}^{2}=

\displaystyle a\mathcal{I}_{{\mathrm{}}{\mathrm{}}}+\left(1-a\right)\mathcal{N}_{{\mathrm{}n}{\mathrm{}}},

\displaystyle 0\leq

\displaystyle a\leq 1,

(C.273)

where
a
is determined by the precise structure of
\mathcal{N}_{{\mathrm{}n}{\mathrm{}}}
(our numerical results use
a=1
).

) and (

\displaystyle e_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{G}}=e_{{\mathrm{RE}}{\mathrm{}}}+e_{{\mathrm{SN}}{\mathrm{}}}.

C.2
Linear Mitigation

Linear mitigation obtains a mitigated observable estimate from a linear combination of noisy observable estimates:

\displaystyle\left\langle\tilde{O}\right\rangle_{\mathrm{M}}=\sum_{i=0}^{N_{\mathrm{C}}}c_{\mathrm{Gl};i}\left\langle\tilde{O}\right\rangle_{i}=\sum_{i=0}^{N_{\mathrm{C}}}c_{\mathrm{Gl};i}\mathrm{Tr}\left[\hat{O}\tilde{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathrm{}};{\mathrm{}i}}\left(\rho_{0}\right)\right]=\mathrm{Tr}\left[\hat{O}\tilde{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathrm{}};{\mathrm{M}}}\left(\rho_{0}\right)\right],

(C.278)

where
\left\langle\tilde{O}\right\rangle_{\mathrm{M}}
is the mitigated observable,
N_{\mathrm{C}}
is the number of extra circuit variants we use in the mitigation (generally
\tilde{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathrm{}0}{\mathrm{}}}
is the original noisy circuit),
c_{\mathrm{Gl};i}
is the coefficient used for the
i
th noisy observable estimate (or equivalently the
i
th noisy circuit variant),
\hat{O}
is the observable we wish to measure,
\tilde{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathrm{}};{\mathrm{}i}}
is the (map corresponding to) the
i
th noisy circuit variant,
\rho_{0}
is the initial state our circuit acts on, and
\tilde{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathrm{}};{\mathrm{M}}}
is the effective mitigated circuit.

C.2.1
Scope

Mitigation methods can have different scopes:

•

Synchronous methods use the same variant generation strategy for each gate in a given variant circuit:

(C.279)

where
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{}};{\mathrm{SM}}}

(C.280)

where
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{}};{\mathrm{AM}}}
is the (channel equivalent to the) asynchronously mitigated circuit,
c_{\mathrm{Gl};j}
is the coefficient of the
j
th noisy circuit variant,
\tilde{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathrm{}};{\mathrm{}j}}
is the
j
th noisy circuit variant,
N_{\mathrm{C}}
is the number of circuit variants used,
\tilde{U}_{{\mathrm{}n};{\mathrm{}J_{n}\left(j\right)}}
is the
J_{n}\left(j\right)
th variant of the
n
th gate. In this case
J_{n}
is some function of
j
that depends on
n
and it need not be the case that
J_{n}\left(j\right)=J_{m}\left(j\right)
if
n\neq m
.

•

Local methods effectively mitigate each gate independently and then these mitigated gates are concatenated in order to form circuit variants and their coefficients:

,\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ 65.0pt-\heightof{\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{U}_{{\mathrm{}n}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}}/2\hskip 72.0pt\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{U}_{{\mathrm{}n}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.63959){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\smash{\begin{tabular}[t]{l}\noindent\hskip 42.67912pt\scalebox{8}{$\displaystyle\sim$}\end{tabular}}}}\put(0.77218,0.10885){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip-17.07182pt\par\noindent\hskip 8.5359pt\scalebox{5}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathrm{}i}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateM.pdf}}\end{picture}}\end{matrix}\Big\}_{i=0}^{N_{\mathrm{L},\mathrm{C},n}}\hskip-24.0pt\begin{matrix}\scalebox{0.1}{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateMWireVS.pdf}}\put(1.60476,1.70547){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[335.53949pt]\end{minipage}}}\end{picture}}\end{matrix}\penalty\ ,

(C.281)

where
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathrm{}};{\mathrm{LM}}}
is the (channel equivalent to the) locally mitigated circuit;
c_{\mathrm{Gl};j}
is the coefficient of the
j
th noisy circuit variant;
\tilde{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathrm{}};{\mathrm{}j}}
is the
j
th noisy circuit variant;
N_{\mathrm{C}}
is the number of circuit variants used;
\tilde{U}_{{\mathrm{}n};{\mathrm{}J_{n}\!\left(j\right)}}
is the
J_{n}\left(j\right)
th variant of the
n
th gate. For local mitigation:

\displaystyle J_{n}\!\left(j\right)=

\displaystyle\left\lfloor\frac{j}{N_{{\mathrm{C},n-1}{\mathrm{}}}+1}\right\rfloor\bmod{\left(N_{{\mathrm{L,C},n}{\mathrm{}}}+1\right)},

\displaystyle N_{{\mathrm{C},n}{\mathrm{}}}=

\displaystyle\left(\prod_{m=1}^{n}\left(N_{\mathrm{L},\mathrm{C},m}+1\right)\right)-1,

(C.282)

where
N_{{\mathrm{C},n}{\mathrm{}}}
is the number of circuits required to mitigate
n
gates (
N_{\mathrm{C}}=N_{{\mathrm{C},N_{\mathrm{G}}}{\mathrm{}}}
, where
N_{\mathrm{G}}
is the number of gates in the circuit),
N_{{\mathrm{L,C},n}{\mathrm{}}}
is the number of noisy variants we use to mitigate the
n
th noisy gate,
\left\lfloor x/y\right\rfloor
is the integer part of the division of
x
by
y
, and
x\bmod{y}
is the remainder of
x
divided by
y
.
\tilde{U}_{{\mathrm{}n};{\mathrm{M}}}
is the mitigated version of the
n
th gate;
c_{\mathrm{L};n,i}
is the coefficient of the
i
th variant of the
n
th gate; and
\tilde{U}_{{\mathrm{}n};{\mathrm{}i}}
is the
i
th noisy variant of the
n
th gate. The global circuit coefficients are defined by:

\displaystyle c_{\mathrm{Gl};j}=\prod_{n=1}^{N_{\mathrm{G}}}c_{\mathrm{L};n,J_{n}\left(j\right)}.

(C.283)

C.2.2
Mitigation Method Implementation

We implement our mitigation methods using Monte Carlo sampling
[
35
]
.

For a global mitigation method we have a mitigated circuit given by:

\displaystyle\begin{matrix}\scalebox{0.1}{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateMWireless.pdf}}\put(0.40119,0.42637){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[335.53992pt]\raggedright\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.60885){\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip 65.0pt-\heightof{\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathrm{}}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}}/2\hskip 72.0pt\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathrm{}}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.63959){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\smash{\begin{tabular}[t]{l}\noindent\hskip 42.67912pt\scalebox{8}{$\displaystyle\sim$}\end{tabular}}}}\put(0.77218,0.10885){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip-17.07182pt\par\noindent\hskip 8.5359pt\scalebox{5}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathrm{M}}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\end{picture}}\end{matrix}\penalty\ =

\displaystyle\penalty\ \Big\{c_{\mathrm{Gl};j}:\begin{matrix}\scalebox{0.1}{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateMWireless.pdf}}\put(0.40119,0.42637){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[335.53992pt]\raggedright\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.60885){\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip 65.0pt-\heightof{\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathrm{}}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}}/2\hskip 72.0pt\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathrm{}}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.63959){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\smash{\begin{tabular}[t]{l}\noindent\hskip 42.67912pt\scalebox{8}{$\displaystyle\sim$}\end{tabular}}}}\put(0.77218,0.10885){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip-17.07182pt\par\noindent\hskip 8.5359pt\scalebox{5}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathrm{}j}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\end{picture}}\end{matrix}\Big\}_{j=0}^{N_{\mathrm{C}}},

\displaystyle C_{\mathrm{Gl}}=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{N_{\mathrm{C}}}\left|c_{\mathrm{Gl};i}\right|,

\displaystyle q_{\mathrm{Gl};j}=

\displaystyle\frac{\left|c_{\mathrm{Gl};j}\right|}{C_{\mathrm{Gl}}},

\displaystyle s_{\mathrm{Gl};j}=

\displaystyle\frac{c_{\mathrm{Gl};j}}{\left|c_{\mathrm{Gl};j}\right|}.

(C.284)

where
\tilde{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathrm{}};{\mathrm{M}}}
is the effective mitigated circuit,
c_{\mathrm{Gl};j}
is the coefficient of the
j
th circuit variant (
\tilde{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathrm{}};{\mathrm{}j}}
),
C_{\mathrm{Gl}}
is the sampling cost,
q_{\mathrm{Gl};j}
is the probability we select the
j
th circuit for a given run of the mitigated circuit, and
s_{\mathrm{Gl};j}
is the sign associated with the
j
th circuit. To implement this mitigated circuit we make
N_{\mathrm{R}}
runs of the circuit, for each run we select at random from the mitigation method’s component circuit variants, selecting circuit
j
with probability
q_{\mathrm{Gl};j}
. We multiply the output of each run by
s_{\mathrm{Gl};j}
(where
j
matches the circuit we implement for that run). We average all the
N_{\mathrm{R}}
(signed) outputs we obtain and multiply the result by
C_{\mathrm{Gl}}
to obtain our mitigated observable estimate.

For a local mitigation method we have a mitigated circuit given by:

\displaystyle\begin{matrix}\scalebox{0.1}{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateMWireless.pdf}}\put(0.40119,0.42637){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[335.53992pt]\raggedright\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.60885){\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip 65.0pt-\heightof{\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathrm{}}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}}/2\hskip 72.0pt\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathrm{}}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.63959){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\smash{\begin{tabular}[t]{l}\noindent\hskip 42.67912pt\scalebox{8}{$\displaystyle\sim$}\end{tabular}}}}\put(0.77218,0.10885){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip-17.07182pt\par\noindent\hskip 8.5359pt\scalebox{5}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathrm{M}}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\end{picture}}\end{matrix}\penalty\ =

\displaystyle\penalty\ \Big(\begin{matrix}\scalebox{0.1}{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateM.pdf}}\put(0.40119,0.42637){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[335.53992pt]\raggedright\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.60885){\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip 65.0pt-\heightof{\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{U}_{{\mathrm{}n}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}}/2\hskip 72.0pt\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{U}_{{\mathrm{}n}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.63959){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\smash{\begin{tabular}[t]{l}\noindent\hskip 42.67912pt\scalebox{8}{$\displaystyle\sim$}\end{tabular}}}}\put(0.77218,0.10885){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip-17.07182pt\par\noindent\hskip 8.5359pt\scalebox{5}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathrm{M}}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateM.pdf}}\end{picture}}\end{matrix}\Big)_{n=1}^{N_{\mathrm{G}}},

\displaystyle\begin{matrix}\scalebox{0.1}{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateM.pdf}}\put(0.40119,0.42637){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[335.53992pt]\raggedright\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.60885){\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip 65.0pt-\heightof{\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{U}_{{\mathrm{}n}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}}/2\hskip 72.0pt\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{U}_{{\mathrm{}n}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.63959){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\smash{\begin{tabular}[t]{l}\noindent\hskip 42.67912pt\scalebox{8}{$\displaystyle\sim$}\end{tabular}}}}\put(0.77218,0.10885){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip-17.07182pt\par\noindent\hskip 8.5359pt\scalebox{5}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathrm{M}}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateM.pdf}}\end{picture}}\end{matrix}=

\displaystyle\begin{matrix}\scalebox{0.1}{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateMWireVS.pdf}}\put(1.60476,1.70547){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[335.53949pt]\end{minipage}}}\end{picture}}\end{matrix}\Big\{c_{\mathrm{L};n,i}:\begin{matrix}\scalebox{0.1}{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateM.pdf}}\put(0.40119,0.42637){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[335.53992pt]\raggedright\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.60885){\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip 65.0pt-\heightof{\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{U}_{{\mathrm{}n}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}}/2\hskip 72.0pt\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{U}_{{\mathrm{}n}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.63959){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\smash{\begin{tabular}[t]{l}\noindent\hskip 42.67912pt\scalebox{8}{$\displaystyle\sim$}\end{tabular}}}}\put(0.77218,0.10885){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip-17.07182pt\par\noindent\hskip 8.5359pt\scalebox{5}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathrm{}i}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateM.pdf}}\end{picture}}\end{matrix}\Big\}_{i=0}^{N_{\mathrm{L},\mathrm{C},n}}\hskip-24.0pt\begin{matrix}\scalebox{0.1}{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateMWireVS.pdf}}\put(1.60476,1.70547){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[335.53949pt]\end{minipage}}}\end{picture}}\end{matrix}\penalty\ ,

\displaystyle C_{\mathrm{L},n}=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{N_{\mathrm{L},\mathrm{C},n}}\left|c_{\mathrm{L};n,i}\right|,

\displaystyle q_{\mathrm{L};n,j}=

\displaystyle\frac{\left|c_{\mathrm{L};n,j}\right|}{C_{\mathrm{L},n}},

\displaystyle s_{\mathrm{L};n,j}=

\displaystyle\frac{c_{\mathrm{L};n,j}}{\left|c_{\mathrm{L};n,j}\right|},

\displaystyle C_{\mathrm{Gl}}=

\displaystyle\prod_{n=1}^{N_{\mathrm{G}}}C_{\mathrm{L},n}.

(C.285)

where
\tilde{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathrm{}};{\mathrm{M}}}
is the effective mitigated circuit,
\tilde{U}_{{\mathrm{}n};{\mathrm{M}}}
is the
n
th effective mitigated gate in the circuit,
N_{\mathrm{G}}
is the number of gates in the circuit,
c_{\mathrm{L};n,i}
is the coefficient of the
i
th noisy gate variant of the
n
th gate (
\tilde{U}_{{\mathrm{}n};{\mathrm{}j}}
),
N_{\mathrm{L},\mathrm{C},n}
is the number of gate variants we use for the
n
th gate,
C_{\mathrm{L},n}
is the local sampling cost of the
n
th gate,
q_{\mathrm{L};n,j}
is the probability we select the
j
th variant for the
n
th gate,
s_{\mathrm{L};n,j}
is the sign associated with the
j
th variant of the
n
th gate, and
C_{\mathrm{Gl}}
is the global sampling cost. To implement this mitigated circuit we make
N_{\mathrm{R}}
runs of the circuit. For each gate in each run we select at random from the mitigated gate’s component gate variants, selecting variant
j
with probability
q_{\mathrm{L};n,j}
(for the
n
th gate). We multiply the output of each run by the product of the signs associated for each gate using in that run, so for the
m
th run we have:

\displaystyle\prod_{n=1}^{N_{\mathrm{G}}}s_{\mathrm{L};n,j_{n,m}},

(C.286)

where
j_{n,m}
is the index of the gate variant selected for the
n
th gate for the
m
th run of the circuit. We take the average of the
N_{\mathrm{R}}
signed outputs and multiply the result by
C_{\mathrm{Gl}}
to obtain our mitigated observable estimate.

C.3
CHILM

Custom-channel-and-hidden-inverse-assisted local mitigation (CHILM) is a local method (see App.
C.2.1
) that uses a mixture of custom channels and hidden inverses to amplify the noise in the noisy gate variants:

\displaystyle\begin{matrix}\scalebox{0.1}{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateM.pdf}}\put(0.40119,0.42637){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[335.53992pt]\raggedright\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.60885){\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip 65.0pt-\heightof{\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{U}_{{\mathrm{}n}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}}/2\hskip 72.0pt\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{U}_{{\mathrm{}n}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.63959){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\smash{\begin{tabular}[t]{l}\noindent\hskip 42.67912pt\scalebox{8}{$\displaystyle\sim$}\end{tabular}}}}\put(0.77218,0.10885){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip-17.07182pt\par\noindent\hskip 8.5359pt\scalebox{5}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathrm{}N_{\mathrm{L},\mathrm{C},n}}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateM.pdf}}\end{picture}}\end{matrix}\penalty\ \penalty\ =

\displaystyle\penalty\ \begin{matrix}\scalebox{0.1}{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateM.pdf}}\put(0.40119,0.42637){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[335.53992pt]\raggedright\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.60885){\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip 65.0pt-\heightof{\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{U}_{{\mathrm{}n}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}}/2\hskip 72.0pt\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{U}_{{\mathrm{}n}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.63959){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\smash{\begin{tabular}[t]{l}\noindent\hskip 42.67912pt\scalebox{8}{$\displaystyle\sim$}\end{tabular}}}}\put(0.77218,0.10885){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip-17.07182pt\par\noindent\hskip 8.5359pt\scalebox{5}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathrm{HI}}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateM.pdf}}\end{picture}}\end{matrix},

(C.288)

where
\tilde{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathrm{}};{\mathrm{M}}}
is the mitigated circuit,
\tilde{U}_{{\mathrm{}n};{\mathrm{M}}}
is
n
th mitigated gate,
N_{\mathrm{G}}
is the number of gates in the circuit,
c_{\mathrm{L};n,j}
is the mitigation coefficient for the
j
th gate variant of the
n
th gate,
\tilde{U}_{{\mathrm{}n};{\mathrm{}j}}
is the
j
th gate variant of the
n
th gate,
N_{\mathrm{L},\mathrm{C},n}
is the number of gate variants for the
n
th gate,
\tilde{U}_{{\mathrm{}n}{\mathrm{}}}
is the
n
th noisy gate before mitigation,
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{}n};{\mathrm{C},j}}
is the custom noise channel used to generate the
j
th variant of the
n
th noisy gate, and
\tilde{U}_{{\mathrm{}n};{\mathrm{HI}}}
is the hidden inverse of the
n
th noisy gate (used for the final of the
N_{\mathrm{L},\mathrm{C},n}
noisy gate variants, not counting the original noisy gate).

CHILM is a special instantiation of probabilistic error cancellation (see Ref.
[
16
]
). The use of custom channels for variant generation is discussed in Ref.
[
23
]
and hidden inverses are introduced in Refs
[
21
,
22
]
(but they are not used therein for probabilistic error cancellation).

The execution time for the
i
th variant of the
n
th gate is assumed to be:

\displaystyle\tau_{{\mathrm{L},n};{\mathrm{}i}}=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\tau_{{\mathrm{L},n}{\mathrm{}}},&\text{ for }i=0,\\
2\tau_{{\mathrm{L},n}{\mathrm{}}},&\text{ for }0<i<N_{\mathrm{L},\mathrm{C},n},\\
\tau_{{\mathrm{L},n}{\mathrm{}}},&\text{ for }i=N_{\mathrm{L},\mathrm{C},n},\end{cases}

(C.289)

where
\tau_{{\mathrm{L},n}{\mathrm{}}}
is the execution time for the original
n
th noisy gate (
\tilde{U}_{{\mathrm{}n};{\mathrm{}0}}=\tilde{U}_{{\mathrm{}n}{\mathrm{}}}
). That is, we assume that the execution time of every custom error channel is the same as the original gate and we assume the execution time of the hidden inverse is the same as the original noisy gate.

We do not implement CHILM for stochastic noise.

For rotational errors we use
N_{\mathrm{L},\mathrm{C},n}=2
where:

\begin{matrix}\scalebox{0.1}{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateM.pdf}}\put(0.40119,0.42637){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[335.53992pt]\raggedright\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.60885){\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip 65.0pt-\heightof{\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\penalty\ \mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\frac{\phi}{2}P_{n}}}_{{\mathrm{}}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}}/2\hskip 72.0pt\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\penalty\ \mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\frac{\phi}{2}P_{n}}}_{{\mathrm{}}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.63959){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\smash{\begin{tabular}[t]{l}\end{tabular}}}}\put(0.954,0.10885){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip-17.07182pt\par\noindent\hskip 8.5359pt\scalebox{5}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathrm{}}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateMVVL.pdf}}\end{picture}}\end{matrix},

(C.290)

with the coefficients:

\displaystyle c_{\mathrm{L};0}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2},

\displaystyle c_{\mathrm{L};1}=

\displaystyle-\frac{1-\cos\phi}{2\cos\phi}=-\frac{\phi^{2}}{4}+\mathcal{O}_{0}\left(\phi^{4}\right),

\displaystyle c_{\mathrm{L};2}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\cos\phi}=\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\phi^{2}}{4}+\mathcal{O}_{0}\left(\phi^{4}\right),

(C.291)

where
\phi
is the noise amplitude of the original noisy gate (see App.
C.1
). This method is unbiased so the proxy bias is zero.

For (stochastic) over-rotation errors we use the same variant generation strategy as for rotational errors (
N_{\mathrm{L},\mathrm{C},n}=2
):

\begin{matrix}\scalebox{0.1}{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateM.pdf}}\put(0.40119,0.42637){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[335.53992pt]\raggedright\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.60885){\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip 65.0pt-\heightof{\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{P_{n}}_{{\mathrm{}}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}}/2\hskip 72.0pt\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{P_{n}}_{{\mathrm{}}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.63959){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\smash{\begin{tabular}[t]{l}\end{tabular}}}}\put(0.77218,0.10885){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip-17.07182pt\par\noindent\hskip 8.5359pt\scalebox{5}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathrm{}}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateM.pdf}}\end{picture}}\end{matrix}\penalty\ &\text{ with prob. }p\end{cases},

(C.292)

and the coefficients:

\displaystyle c_{\mathrm{L};0}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2},\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ c_{\mathrm{L};1}=-\frac{1-\left(1-2p\right)\cos\phi}{2\left(1-2p\right)\cos\phi}=-p-\frac{\phi^{2}}{4}+\mathcal{O}_{0}\left(p^{2},\phi^{2}p,\phi^{4}\right),

(C.293)

\displaystyle c_{\mathrm{L};2}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\left(1-2p\right)\cos\phi}=\frac{1}{2}+p+\frac{\phi^{2}}{4}+\mathcal{O}_{0}\left(p^{2},\phi^{2}p,\phi^{4}\right),

(C.294)

where
p
and
\phi
are the noise amplitudes of the stochastic and rotational components of the original noisy gate’s error channel, respectively (see App.
C.1
). This method is unbiased so the proxy bias is zero. It is worth noting that the hidden inverse used in the mitigation of stochastic over-rotation errors has a noise channel that is not the inverse of the noise channel of the original noisy gate. Only the coherent (rotational) part of the noise channel is inverted. The stochastic component is assumed to be the same as the original gate.

C.4
CHISM

Custom-channel-and-hidden-inverse-assisted synchronous mitigation (CHISM) is a synchronous method (see App.
C.2.1
) that uses a mixture of custom channels and hidden inverses to amplify the noise in the noisy gate variants:

(C.295)

8.5359pt\scalebox{5}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathrm{}N_{\mathrm{C}}}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateM.pdf}}\end{picture}}\end{matrix}\penalty\ =\penalty\ \begin{matrix}\scalebox{0.1}{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateM.pdf}}\put(0.40119,0.42637){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[335.53992pt]\raggedright\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.60885){\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip 65.0pt-\heightof{\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{U}_{{\mathrm{}n}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}}/2\hskip 72.0pt\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{U}_{{\mathrm{}n}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.63959){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\smash{\begin{tabular}[t]{l}\noindent\hskip 42.67912pt\scalebox{8}{$\displaystyle\sim$}\end{tabular}}}}\put(0.77218,0.10885){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip-17.07182pt\par\noindent\hskip 8.5359pt\scalebox{5}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathrm{HI}}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateM.pdf}}\end{picture}}\end{matrix},

(C.296)

where
\tilde{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathrm{}};{\mathrm{M}}}
is the mitigated circuit,
c_{\mathrm{Gl};j}
is the mitigation coefficient for the
j
th circuit variant,
\tilde{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathrm{}};{\mathrm{}j}}
is the
j
th circuit variant,
N_{\mathrm{C}}
is the number of circuit variants,
\tilde{U}_{{\mathrm{}n};{\mathrm{}j}}
is the
j
th variant of the
n
th noisy gate,
N_{\mathrm{G}}
is the number of gates in the circuit,
\tilde{U}_{{\mathrm{}n}{\mathrm{}}}
is the
n
th noisy gate before mitigation,
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{}n};{\mathrm{C},j}}
is the custom noise channel used to generate the
j
th variant of the
n
th noisy gate, and
\tilde{U}_{{\mathrm{}n};{\mathrm{HI}}}
is the hidden inverse of the
n
th noisy gate (used for the final of the
N_{\mathrm{C}}
noisy gate variants, not counting the original noisy gate).

CHISM requires all the mitigated noisy gates to have noise of the same type with the same noise amplitude. CHISM is a generalisation of the zero-noise extrapolation methods of Refs
[
16
,
23
]
to target rotational errors. The use of custom channels for variant generation is discussed in Ref.
[
23
]
and hidden inverses are introduced in Refs
[
21
,
22
]
.

The execution time for the
j
th circuit is given by:

\displaystyle\tau_{\mathrm{E},j}=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\tau_{\mathrm{E}},&\text{ for }i=0,\\
2\tau_{\mathrm{E}},&\text{ for }0<i<N_{\mathrm{C}},\\
\tau_{\mathrm{E}},&\text{ for }i=N_{\mathrm{C}},\end{cases}

(C.297)

where
\tau_{\mathrm{E}}
is the execution time for the original noisy circuit (
\tilde{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathrm{}};{\mathrm{}0}}=\tilde{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathrm{}}{\mathrm{}}}
). We assume that the execution time of every custom error channel is the same as the original gate and that all the gates take the same time to implement. We also assume the execution time of the hidden inverse is the same as the original noisy gate.

We do not implement CHISM for stochastic noise.

For rotational errors we use the gate variants:

\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\frac{\phi}{2}P_{n}}}_{{\mathrm{}}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.63959){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\smash{\begin{tabular}[t]{l}\end{tabular}}}}\put(0.954,0.10885){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip-17.07182pt\par\noindent\hskip 8.5359pt\scalebox{5}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathrm{}}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateMVVL.pdf}}\end{picture}}\end{matrix},

(C.298)

which means we have the amplified noise amplitudes:

\displaystyle\phi_{0}=

\displaystyle\phi,

\displaystyle\phi_{0<i<N_{\mathrm{C}}}=

\displaystyle\phi+\frac{2i}{N_{\mathrm{C}}}\uppi,

\displaystyle\phi_{N_{\mathrm{C}}}=

\displaystyle-\phi.

(C.299)

We use the coefficients:

\displaystyle c_{\mathrm{Gl};i}=

\displaystyle\prod_{j=0,j\neq i}^{N_{\mathrm{C}}}\frac{\sin\frac{\phi_{j}}{2}}{\sin\frac{\phi_{j}-\phi_{i}}{2}},

(C.300)

where
\phi
is the noise amplitude of the original noisy gate (see App.
C.1
) and
N_{\mathrm{C}}
is the number of gates used for the mitigation. We do not have an analytic form for the proxy bias in general but if we set
N_{\mathrm{C}}=2N_{\mathrm{G}}
(as we do in the main text) the method is unbiased.

C.5
CIILM

Custom-channel-and-identity-insertion-assisted local mitigation (CIILM) is a local method (see App.
C.2.1
) that uses custom channels and identity insertions to amplify the noise in the noisy gate variants:

=\penalty\ \begin{matrix}\scalebox{0.1}{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateM.pdf}}\put(0.40119,0.42637){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[335.53992pt]\raggedright\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.60885){\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip 65.0pt-\heightof{\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}_{{\mathrm{}n}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}}/2\hskip 72.0pt\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}_{{\mathrm{}n}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.63959){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\smash{\begin{tabular}[t]{l}\end{tabular}}}}\put(0.77218,0.10885){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip-17.07182pt\par\noindent\hskip 8.5359pt\scalebox{5}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathrm{C}}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateM.pdf}}\end{picture}}\end{matrix},

(C.302)

where
\tilde{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathrm{}};{\mathrm{M}}}
is the mitigated circuit,
\tilde{U}_{{\mathrm{}n};{\mathrm{M}}}
is
n
th mitigated gate,
N_{\mathrm{G}}
is the number of gates in the circuit,
c_{\mathrm{L};n,j}
is the mitigation coefficient for the
j
th gate variant of the
n
th gate,
\tilde{U}_{{\mathrm{}n};{\mathrm{}j}}
is the
j
th gate variant of the
n
th gate,
N_{\mathrm{L},\mathrm{C},n}
is the number of gate variants for the
n
th gate,
\tilde{U}_{{\mathrm{}n};{\mathrm{T}}}
is the tailored version of the
n
th noisy gate,
\widetilde{{U}^{\dagger}}_{{\mathrm{}n};{\mathrm{T}}}
is the tailored version of the noisy inverse of the
n
th gate (assumed to have the same error channel as
\tilde{U}_{{\mathrm{}n};{\mathrm{T}}}
),
\tilde{U}_{{\mathrm{}n}{\mathrm{}}}
is the
n
th noisy gate before mitigation,
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{}n};{\mathrm{C}}}
is the custom noise channel used to tailor the
n
th noisy gate, and
\widetilde{{U}^{\dagger}}_{{\mathrm{}n}{\mathrm{}}}
is the noisy inverse of the
n
th noisy gate. To save time in characterisation one need not ever characterise the gates
\tilde{U}_{{\mathrm{}n}{\mathrm{}}}
and
\widetilde{{U}^{\dagger}}_{{\mathrm{}n}{\mathrm{}}}
since the control channel can be chosen independently of the noise amplitude. It suffices to characterise
\tilde{U}_{{\mathrm{}n};{\mathrm{T}}}
and
\widetilde{{U}^{\dagger}}_{{\mathrm{}n};{\mathrm{T}}}
.

The version of CIILM presented here requires the error channels associated with the noisy gate and its noisy inverse to be the same and that the noisy gate, the noisy inverse, their error channels, and the custom error channel all commute with each other. CIILM could be considered to be an example of NEPEC (see Ref.
[
18
]
) a variant of probabilistic error cancellation (see Ref.
[
16
]
). The use of custom channels for noise amplification is discussed in Ref.
[
23
]
.

The execution time for the
i
th variant of the
n
th gate is assumed to be:

\displaystyle\tau_{{\mathrm{L},n};{\mathrm{}i}}=

\displaystyle 2\left(2i+1\right)\tau_{{\mathrm{L},n}{\mathrm{}}},

(C.303)

where
\tau_{{\mathrm{L},n}{\mathrm{}}}
is the execution time for the original
n
th noisy gate (
\tilde{U}_{{\mathrm{}n}{\mathrm{}}}
). That is, we assume that the execution time of every custom error channel is the same as the original gate and that the forward and inverse gates have the same execution time.

We do not implement CIILM for stochastic noise.

For rotational errors we use
N_{\mathrm{L},\mathrm{C},n}=2
and the custom channel is given by:

\displaystyle\begin{matrix}\scalebox{0.1}{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateM.pdf}}\put(0.40119,0.42637){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[335.53992pt]\raggedright\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.60885){\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip 65.0pt-\heightof{\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}_{{\mathrm{}n}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}}/2\hskip 72.0pt\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}_{{\mathrm{}n}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.63959){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\smash{\begin{tabular}[t]{l}\end{tabular}}}}\put(0.77218,0.10885){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip-17.07182pt\par\noindent\hskip 8.5359pt\scalebox{5}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathrm{C}}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateM.pdf}}\end{picture}}\end{matrix}\penalty\ =

\displaystyle\penalty\ \begin{matrix}\scalebox{0.1}{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateMVVL.pdf}}\put(0.40119,0.42637){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[335.53992pt]\raggedright\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.60885){\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip 65.0pt-\heightof{\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\penalty\ \mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\uppi}{3}P_{n}}}_{{\mathrm{}}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}}/2\hskip 72.0pt\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\penalty\ \mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\uppi}{3}P_{n}}}_{{\mathrm{}}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.63959){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\smash{\begin{tabular}[t]{l}\end{tabular}}}}\put(0.954,0.10885){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip-17.07182pt\par\noindent\hskip 8.5359pt\scalebox{5}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathrm{}}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateMVVL.pdf}}\end{picture}}\end{matrix},

(C.304)

where
P_{n}
is the generator of the rotational error of the
n
th noisy gate. The coefficients are given by:

\displaystyle c_{\mathrm{L};0}=

\displaystyle\frac{\sin\frac{\phi_{1}}{2}\sin\frac{\phi_{2}}{2}}{\sin\frac{\phi_{1}-\phi_{0}}{2}\sin\frac{\phi_{2}-\phi_{0}}{2}}=-\sqrt{3}\phi+\mathcal{O}_{0}\left(\phi^{2}\right),

(C.305)

\displaystyle c_{\mathrm{L};1}=

\displaystyle\frac{\sin\frac{\phi_{0}}{2}\sin\frac{\phi_{2}}{2}}{\sin\frac{\phi_{0}-\phi_{1}}{2}\sin\frac{\phi_{2}-\phi_{1}}{2}}=1+\mathcal{O}_{0}\left(\phi^{2}\right),

(C.306)

\displaystyle c_{\mathrm{L};2}=

\displaystyle\frac{\sin\frac{\phi_{0}}{2}\sin\frac{\phi_{1}}{2}}{\sin\frac{\phi_{0}-\phi_{2}}{2}\sin\frac{\phi_{1}-\phi_{2}}{2}}=\sqrt{3}\phi+\mathcal{O}_{0}\left(\phi^{2}\right),

(C.307)

where the effective noise amplitudes are given by:

\displaystyle\phi_{0}=

\displaystyle\frac{2\uppi}{3}+\phi,

\displaystyle\phi_{1}=

\displaystyle 2\uppi+3\phi,

\displaystyle\phi_{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{10\uppi}{3}+5\phi,

(C.308)

and
\phi
is the noise amplitude of the original noisy gate (see App.
C.1
). This method is unbiased so the proxy bias is zero.

C.6
CLM

Custom-channel-assisted local mitigation (CLM) is a local method (see App.
C.2.1
) that uses custom channels to amplify the noise in the noisy gate variants:

(C.310)

where
\tilde{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathrm{}};{\mathrm{M}}}
is the mitigated circuit,
\tilde{U}_{{\mathrm{}n};{\mathrm{M}}}
is
n
th mitigated gate,
N_{\mathrm{G}}
is the number of gates in the circuit,
c_{\mathrm{L};n,j}
is the mitigation coefficient for the
j
th gate variant of the
n
th gate,
\tilde{U}_{{\mathrm{}n};{\mathrm{}j}}
is the
j
th gate variant of the
n
th gate,
N_{\mathrm{L},\mathrm{C},n}
is the number of gate variants for the
n
th gate and the order of the mitigation used for that gate,
\tilde{U}_{{\mathrm{}n}{\mathrm{}}}
is the
n
th noisy gate before mitigation,
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{}n};{\mathrm{C},j}}
is the custom noise channel used to generate the
j
th variant of the
n
th noisy gate,
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{}n}{\mathrm{}}}
is the original error channel of the
n
th noisy gate,
U_{{\mathrm{}n}{\mathrm{}}}
is the noise-free component of the
n
th gate, and
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{}n};{\mathrm{}j}}
is the error channel of the
j
th variant of the
n
th gate.

CLM could be considered to be an example of NEPEC (see Ref.
[
18
]
). CLM:SN (used to mitigate stochastic noise) is functionally equivalent to the probabilistic error cancellation procedure that is declared optimal for dephasing noise in Ref.
[
6
]
. Though, in fact, it is possible to obtain a slightly lower sampling cost—as noted by Ref.
[
3
]
, but the difference is negligible.

The execution time for the
i
th variant of the
n
th gate is assumed to be:

\displaystyle\tau_{{\mathrm{L},n};{\mathrm{}i}}=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\tau_{{\mathrm{L},n}{\mathrm{}}},&\text{ for }i=0,\\
2\tau_{{\mathrm{L},n}{\mathrm{}}},&\text{ for }i>0,\\
\end{cases}

(C.311)

where
\tau_{{\mathrm{L},n}{\mathrm{}}}
is the execution time for the original
n
th noisy gate (
\tilde{U}_{{\mathrm{}n};{\mathrm{}0}}=\tilde{U}_{{\mathrm{}n}{\mathrm{}}}
). That is, we assume that the execution time of every custom error channel is the same as the original gate. An exception is the case of (stochastic) over-rotation errors where we break our custom channels into Clifford gates and assume that each Clifford gate has the same execution time (see below).

For stochastic noise we use
N_{\mathrm{L},\mathrm{C},n}=1
, with the noisy gate variants:

\begin{matrix}\scalebox{0.1}{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateM.pdf}}\put(0.40119,0.42637){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[335.53992pt]\raggedright\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.60885){\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip 65.0pt-\heightof{\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathcal{N}}_{{\mathrm{}n}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}}/2\hskip 72.0pt\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathcal{N}}_{{\mathrm{}n}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.63959){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\smash{\begin{tabular}[t]{l}\end{tabular}}}}\put(0.77218,0.10885){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip-17.07182pt\par\noindent\hskip 8.5359pt\scalebox{5}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathrm{}}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateM.pdf}}\end{picture}}\end{matrix}\penalty\ &\text{ with probability }\sin^{2}\frac{\uppi}{2},\end{cases}\penalty\

(C.312)

and the corresponding coefficients:

\displaystyle c_{\mathrm{L};0}=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{1}}{p_{1}-p_{0}}=1+p+\mathcal{O}_{0}\left(p^{2}\right),

\displaystyle c_{\mathrm{L};1}=

\displaystyle-\frac{p_{0}}{p_{1}-p_{0}}=-p+\mathcal{O}_{0}\left(p^{2}\right).

(C.313)

where the amplified noise amplitudes are given by:

\displaystyle p_{0}=

\displaystyle p,

\displaystyle p_{1}=

\displaystyle p+\sin^{2}\frac{\uppi}{2}\left(1-\left(1+a\right)p\right),

(C.314)

where
p
is the noise amplitude of the original noisy gate and
0\leq a\leq 1
is determined by the nature of the noise channel (see Eq. (
C.273
)). This method is unbiased so the proxy bias is zero.

N_{\mathrm{L},\mathrm{C},n}=2
and the noisy gate variants:

65.0pt-\heightof{\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\penalty\ \mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{3\uppi}{4}P_{n}}}_{{\mathrm{}}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}}/2\hskip 72.0pt\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\penalty\ \mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{3\uppi}{4}P_{n}}}_{{\mathrm{}}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.63959){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\smash{\begin{tabular}[t]{l}\end{tabular}}}}\put(0.954,0.10885){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip-17.07182pt\par\noindent\hskip 8.5359pt\scalebox{5}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathrm{}}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateMVVL.pdf}}\end{picture}}\end{matrix},

(C.315)

with the coefficients:

\displaystyle c_{\mathrm{L};0}=

\displaystyle\cos\phi=1-\frac{\phi^{2}}{2}+\mathcal{O}_{0}\left(\phi^{4}\right),

\displaystyle c_{\mathrm{L};1}=

\displaystyle-\frac{\cos\phi-1+\sin\phi}{2}=-\frac{\phi}{2}+\frac{\phi^{2}}{4}+\mathcal{O}_{0}\left(\phi^{4}\right),

(C.316)

\displaystyle c_{\mathrm{L};2}=

\displaystyle\frac{1-\cos\phi+\sin\phi}{2}=\frac{\phi}{2}+\frac{\phi^{2}}{4}+\mathcal{O}_{0}\left(\phi^{4}\right),

(C.317)

where
\phi
is the noise amplitude of the original noisy gate (see App.
C.1
). This method is unbiased so the proxy bias is zero.

For (stochastic) over-rotation errors we use
N_{\mathrm{L},\mathrm{C},n}=2
and the same custom channels as for rotational errors (Eq. (
C.315
)). When we make use of this method in the main text we decompose our (non-trivial) custom channels into Clifford gates:

8.5359pt\scalebox{5}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathrm{}}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateM.pdf}}\end{picture}}\end{matrix},

\displaystyle\mathrm{S}_{P_{n}}^{2}=P_{n},

(C.318)

(
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{}n};{\mathrm{C},0}}
is the identity channel) and we assume every gate takes the same amount of time to implement. So:

\displaystyle\tau_{{\mathrm{L},n};{\mathrm{}i}}=

\displaystyle\left(i+1\right)\tau_{{\mathrm{L},n}{\mathrm{}}}.

(C.319)

We use the coefficients:

\displaystyle c_{\mathrm{L};0}=

\displaystyle\frac{\cos\phi}{1-2p}=1+2p-\frac{\phi^{2}}{2}+\mathcal{O}_{0}\left(p^{2},p\phi^{2},\phi^{4}\right),

(C.320)

\displaystyle c_{\mathrm{L};1}=

\displaystyle-\frac{\cos\phi-1+2p+\sin\phi}{2\left(1-2p\right)}=-p-\frac{\phi}{2}+\frac{\phi^{2}}{4}+\mathcal{O}_{0}\left(p^{2},\phi p,\phi^{4}\right),

(C.321)

\displaystyle c_{\mathrm{L};2}=

\displaystyle\frac{1-2p-\cos\phi+\sin\phi}{2\left(1-2p\right)}=-p+\frac{\phi}{2}+\frac{\phi^{2}}{4}+\mathcal{O}_{0}\left(p^{2},\phi p,\phi^{4}\right),

(C.322)

where
p
and
\phi
are the noise amplitudes of the stochastic and rotational components of the original noisy gate’s error channel, respectively (see App.
C.1
). This method is unbiased so the proxy bias is zero.

C.7
CSM

Custom-channel-assisted synchronous mitigation (CSM) is a synchronous method (see App.
C.2.1
) that uses custom channels to amplify the noise in the noisy gate variants:

where
\tilde{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathrm{}};{\mathrm{M}}}
is the mitigated circuit,
c_{\mathrm{Gl};j}
is the mitigation coefficient for the
j
th circuit variant,
\tilde{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathrm{}};{\mathrm{}j}}
is the
j
th circuit variant,
N_{\mathrm{C}}=N_{\mathrm{M}}
is the number of circuit variants and the order of the mitigation,
\tilde{U}_{{\mathrm{}n};{\mathrm{}j}}
is the
j
th variant of the
n
th noisy gate,
N_{\mathrm{G}}
is the number of gates in the circuit,
\tilde{U}_{{\mathrm{}n}{\mathrm{}}}
is the
n
th noisy gate before mitigation,
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{}n};{\mathrm{C},j}}
is the custom noise channel used to generate the
j
th variant of the
n
th noisy gate,
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{}n}{\mathrm{}}}
is the original error channel of the
n
th noisy gate,
U_{{\mathrm{}n}{\mathrm{}}}
is the noise-free component of the
n
th gate, and
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{}n};{\mathrm{}j}}
is the error channel of the
j
th variant of the
n
th gate.

CSM requires all error channels to have the same type (e.g. they must all be stochastic or rotational) and have the same noise amplitude. All the custom channels have the same noise amplitude but may have different generators (their generator should match that of the noise channel they are tailoring). CSM is a zero-noise extrapolation method akin to Refs
[
16
,
23
]
. Akin to Ref.
[
23
]
it uses custom noise channels in order to amplify the noise of gates and produce circuit variants with differing noise levels to use for the mitigation. However, it generalises their approach by adding the ability to perform unbiased mitigation of rotational errors.

The execution time for the
j
th circuit is given by:

\displaystyle\tau_{\mathrm{E},j}=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\tau_{\mathrm{E}},&\text{ for }i=0,\\
2\tau_{\mathrm{E}},&\text{ for }i>0,\\
\end{cases}

(C.325)

where
\tau_{\mathrm{E}}
is the execution time for the original noisy circuit. We assume that the execution time of every custom error channel is the same as the original gate and that all the gates take the same time to implement.

For stochastic noise we use the gate variants:

\displaystyle\begin{matrix}\scalebox{0.1}{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateM.pdf}}\put(0.40119,0.42637){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[335.53992pt]\raggedright\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.60885){\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip 65.0pt-\heightof{\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{U}_{{\mathrm{}n}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}}/2\hskip 72.0pt\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{U}_{{\mathrm{}n}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.63959){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\smash{\begin{tabular}[t]{l}\noindent\hskip 42.67912pt\scalebox{8}{$\displaystyle\sim$}\end{tabular}}}}\put(0.77218,0.10885){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip-17.07182pt\par\noindent\hskip 8.5359pt\scalebox{5}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathrm{}0}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateM.pdf}}\end{picture}}\end{matrix}\penalty\ =\penalty\

\displaystyle\begin{matrix}\scalebox{0.1}{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateM.pdf}}\put(0.40119,0.42637){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[335.53992pt]\raggedright\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.60885){\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip 65.0pt-\heightof{\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{U}_{{\mathrm{}n}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}}/2\hskip 72.0pt\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{U}_{{\mathrm{}n}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.63959){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\smash{\begin{tabular}[t]{l}\noindent\hskip 42.67912pt\scalebox{8}{$\displaystyle\sim$}\end{tabular}}}}\put(0.77218,0.10885){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip-17.07182pt\par\noindent\hskip 8.5359pt\scalebox{5}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathrm{}0<i}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateM.pdf}}\end{picture}}\end{matrix}\penalty\ =\penalty\

}1-\sin^{2}\frac{i\uppi}{2N_{\mathrm{M}}},\\
\begin{matrix}\scalebox{0.1}{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateM.pdf}}\put(0.40119,0.42637){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[335.53992pt]\raggedright\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.60885){\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip 65.0pt-\heightof{\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathcal{N}}_{{\mathrm{}n}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}}/2\hskip 72.0pt\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathcal{N}}_{{\mathrm{}n}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.63959){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\smash{\begin{tabular}[t]{l}\end{tabular}}}}\put(0.77218,0.10885){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip-17.07182pt\par\noindent\hskip 8.5359pt\scalebox{5}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathrm{}}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateM.pdf}}\end{picture}}\end{matrix}\penalty\ &\text{ with probability }\sin^{2}\frac{i\uppi}{2N_{\mathrm{M}}},\end{cases}\penalty\

(C.326)

with the coefficients:

\displaystyle c_{\mathrm{Gl};i}=

\displaystyle\prod_{m=0:m\neq i}^{N_{\mathrm{M}}}\frac{p_{m}}{p_{m}-p_{i}},

\displaystyle p_{m}=

\displaystyle p+\sin^{2}\frac{m\uppi}{2N_{\mathrm{M}}}\left(1-\left(1+a\right)p\right),

(C.327)

where
p
is the noise amplitude of the original noisy gate and
0\leq a\leq 1
is determined by the nature of the noise channel (see Eq. (
C.273
)). We do not have an analytic form for the proxy bias in general but if we set
N_{\mathrm{M}}=N_{\mathrm{G}}
(as we do in the main text) the method is unbiased.

\displaystyle\theta_{0<i}=

\displaystyle\frac{2i-1}{N_{\mathrm{M}}}\uppi.

(C.328)

with the coefficients:

\displaystyle c_{\mathrm{Gl};i}=

\displaystyle\prod_{j=0,j\neq i}^{N_{\mathrm{M}}}\frac{\sin\frac{\phi_{j}}{2}}{\sin\frac{\phi_{j}-\phi_{i}}{2}},

\displaystyle\phi_{0}=

\displaystyle\phi,

\displaystyle\phi_{0<i}=

\displaystyle\phi+\theta_{i},

(C.329)

where
\phi
is the noise amplitude of the original noisy gate (see App.
C.1
) and
N_{\mathrm{M}}
is the order of the mitigation. We do not have an analytic form for the proxy bias in general but if we set
N_{\mathrm{M}}=2N_{\mathrm{G}}
(as we do in the main text) the method is unbiased.

C.8
IIAM

Identity-insertion-assisted asynchronous mitigation (IIAM) is an asynchronous method (see App.
C.2.1
) that uses identity insertions to amplify the noise in the noisy gate variants:

\Big\{c_{\mathrm{Gl};i}:\Big(\begin{matrix}\scalebox{0.1}{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateM.pdf}}\put(0.40119,0.42637){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[335.53992pt]\raggedright\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.60885){\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip \penalty\ \Big)_{n=1}^{N_{\mathrm{G}}}\Big\}_{i=0}^{N_{\mathrm{C}}}\penalty\ ,

(C.330)

\displaystyle\begin{matrix}\scalebox{0.1}{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateM.pdf}}\put(0.40119,0.42637){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[335.53992pt]\raggedright\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.60885){\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip 65.0pt-\heightof{\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{U}_{{\mathrm{}n}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}}/2\hskip 72.0pt\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{U}_{{\mathrm{}n}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.63959){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\smash{\begin{tabular}[t]{l}\noindent\hskip 42.67912pt\scalebox{8}{$\displaystyle\sim$}\end{tabular}}}}\put(0.77218,0.10885){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip-17.07182pt\par\noindent\hskip 8.5359pt\scalebox{5}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathrm{}J_{n}\left(i\right)}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateM.pdf}}\end{picture}}\end{matrix}\penalty\ =\penalty\

\penalty\ ,

(C.331)

where
\tilde{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathrm{}};{\mathrm{M}}}
is the mitigated circuit,
c_{\mathrm{Gl};i}
is the mitigation coefficient for the
i
th circuit variant,
\tilde{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathrm{}};{\mathrm{}i}}
is the
i
th circuit variant,
N_{\mathrm{C}}
is the number of circuit variants,
\tilde{U}_{{\mathrm{}n};{\mathrm{}J_{n}\!\left(i\right)}}
is the
J_{n}\!\left(i\right)
th variant of the
n
th noisy gate,
N_{\mathrm{G}}
is the number of gates in the circuit,
\tilde{U}_{{\mathrm{}n}{\mathrm{}}}
is the
n
th noisy gate before mitigation,
\widetilde{{U}^{\dagger}}_{{\mathrm{}n}{\mathrm{}}}
is the noisy inverse gate,
U_{{\mathrm{}n}{\mathrm{}}}
is the noise-free component of the
n
th gate, and
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{}n};{\mathrm{}J_{n}\!\left(i\right)}}
is the error channel of the
J_{n}\!\left(i\right)
th variant of the
n
th gate.

IIAM requires all error channels to be identical and to commute with the gates they affect. IIAM is a generalisation of RIIM to more diverse noise models, RIIM is introduced in Ref.
[
34
]
(see also Ref.
[
5
]
) as a method for dealing with local depolarising noise. We only have a knowledge-free version of this method.

The definition of
J_{n}\left(i\right)
is rather complex so it is easier to represent the mitigation in another, equivalent way:

\displaystyle\begin{matrix}\scalebox{0.1}{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateMWireless.pdf}}\put(0.40119,0.42637){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[335.53992pt]\raggedright\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.60885){\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip 65.0pt-\heightof{\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathrm{}}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}}/2\hskip 72.0pt\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathrm{}}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.63959){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\smash{\begin{tabular}[t]{l}\noindent\hskip 42.67912pt\scalebox{8}{$\displaystyle\sim$}\end{tabular}}}}\put(0.77218,0.10885){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip-17.07182pt\par\noindent\hskip 8.5359pt\scalebox{5}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathrm{M}}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\end{picture}}\end{matrix}\penalty\ =

\displaystyle\penalty\ \left\{1:\begin{matrix}\scalebox{0.1}{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateMWireless.pdf}}\put(0.40119,0.42637){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[335.53992pt]\raggedright\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.60885){\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip 65.0pt-\heightof{\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathrm{A}}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}}/2\hskip 72.0pt\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathrm{A}}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.63959){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\smash{\begin{tabular}[t]{l}\noindent\hskip 42.67912pt\scalebox{8}{$\displaystyle\sim$}\end{tabular}}}}\put(0.77218,0.10885){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip-17.07182pt\par\noindent\hskip 8.5359pt\scalebox{5}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathrm{}m}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\end{picture}}\end{matrix}\right\}_{m=0}^{N_{\mathrm{M}}}\penalty\ ,

\displaystyle\begin{matrix}\scalebox{0.1}{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateMWireless.pdf}}\put(0.40119,0.42637){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[335.53992pt]\raggedright\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.60885){\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip 65.0pt-\heightof{\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathrm{A}}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}}/2\hskip 72.0pt\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathrm{A}}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.63959){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\smash{\begin{tabular}[t]{l}\noindent\hskip 42.67912pt\scalebox{8}{$\displaystyle\sim$}\end{tabular}}}}\put(0.77218,0.10885){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip-17.07182pt\par\noindent\hskip 8.5359pt\scalebox{5}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathrm{}m}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\end{picture}}\end{matrix}\penalty\ =

\displaystyle\penalty\ \left\{c_{{\mathrm{A},m};{\mathrm{}j}}:\begin{matrix}\scalebox{0.1}{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateMWireless.pdf}}\put(0.40119,0.42637){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[335.53992pt]\raggedright\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.60885){\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip 65.0pt-\heightof{\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\breve{\mathcal{C}}}_{{\mathrm{A}}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}}/2\hskip 72.0pt\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\breve{\mathcal{C}}}_{{\mathrm{A}}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.63959){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\smash{\begin{tabular}[t]{l}\noindent\hskip 42.67912pt\scalebox{8}{$\displaystyle\sim$}\end{tabular}}}}\put(0.77218,0.10885){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip-17.07182pt\par\noindent\hskip 8.5359pt\scalebox{5}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathrm{}m,j}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\end{picture}}\end{matrix}\penalty\ \penalty\ \right\}_{j=0}^{N_{\mathrm{A},m}-1}\penalty\ ,

(C.332)

where
N_{\mathrm{M}}
is the order of the mitigation,
\tilde{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathrm{A}};{\mathrm{}m}}
is a linear combination of all the circuit variants that can be produced by applying exactly
m
identity insertions anywhere in the circuit. This could be
m
identity insertions applied to any one gate,
1
identity insertion applied to any
m
gates, or anything in between.
\tilde{\breve{\mathcal{C}}}_{{\mathrm{A}};{\mathrm{}m,j}}
is a linear combination (with unit coefficients) of the
j
th class of ways to include
m
identity insertions and
N_{\mathrm{A},m}
is the number of distinct configurations of
m
identity insertions that can be applied, i.e. the number of integer partitions of
m
. Each class has a list of instructions associated with it:
L_{{\mathrm{A}};{\mathrm{}m,j}}
, where each element of this list denotes a number of identity insertions followed by a number of gates to apply that number of identity insertions to. For example, we have:

\displaystyle L_{{\mathrm{A}};{\mathrm{}0,0}}=

\displaystyle\left\{\left\{0,0\right\}\right\},

\displaystyle L_{{\mathrm{A}};{\mathrm{}1,0}}=

\displaystyle\left\{\left\{1,1\right\}\right\},

\displaystyle L_{{\mathrm{A}};{\mathrm{}4,3}}=

\displaystyle\left\{\left\{2,1\right\},\left\{1,2\right\}\right\},

\displaystyle L_{{\mathrm{A}};{\mathrm{}5,4}}=

\displaystyle\left\{\left\{2,2\right\},\left\{1,1\right\}\right\},

(C.333)

the first (
L_{{\mathrm{A}};{\mathrm{}0,0}}
) indicates we use no identity insertions so
\tilde{\breve{\mathcal{C}}}_{{\mathrm{A}};{\mathrm{}0,0}}
is just our original noisy circuit (
\tilde{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathrm{}}{\mathrm{}}}
); the second (
L_{{\mathrm{A}};{\mathrm{}1,0}}
) indicates we use one identity insertion so
\tilde{\breve{\mathcal{C}}}_{{\mathrm{A}};{\mathrm{}1,0}}
consists of all the noisy circuits that can be made using a single identity insertion so it is made up of
N_{\mathrm{G}}
distinct circuits, each one applies the identity insertion to a different gate; the third (
L_{{\mathrm{A}};{\mathrm{}4,3}}
) indicates each circuit that makes up
\tilde{\breve{\mathcal{C}}}_{{\mathrm{A}};{\mathrm{}4,3}}
has four identity insertions in total: one gate has two identity insertions and two gates have one identity insertion, we include all possible circuits that satisfy this requirement; and the fourth (
L_{{\mathrm{A}};{\mathrm{}5,4}}
) indicates each circuit that makes up
\tilde{\breve{\mathcal{C}}}_{{\mathrm{A}};{\mathrm{}5,4}}
has five identity insertions in total: two gates have two identity insertions and one gate has one identity insertion, we include all possible circuits that satisfy this requirement. In Table
6
we provide the configuration lists for
m=0
to
m=5
; the multiplicity of
\tilde{\breve{\mathcal{C}}}_{{\mathrm{A}};{\mathrm{}m,j}}
(
\#\left[\tilde{\breve{\mathcal{C}}}_{{\mathrm{A}};{\mathrm{}m,j}}\right]
, i.e. the number of distinct circuits
\tilde{\breve{\mathcal{C}}}_{{\mathrm{A}};{\mathrm{}m,j}}
it is made up of); all the coefficients required for the mitigations; and the equivalent
i
index of each coefficient (see Eq. (
C.330
)):

\displaystyle i=

\displaystyle j+\sum_{n=0}^{m-1}N_{\mathrm{A},n},

\displaystyle c_{{\mathrm{Gl}};{\mathrm{}i}}=

\displaystyle\#\left[\tilde{\breve{\mathcal{C}}}_{{\mathrm{A}};{\mathrm{}m,j}}\right]c_{{\mathrm{A},m};{\mathrm{}j}},

\displaystyle\begin{matrix}\scalebox{0.1}{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateM.pdf}}\put(0.40119,0.42637){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[335.53992pt]\raggedright\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.60885){\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip 65.0pt-\heightof{\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathrm{}}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}}/2\hskip 72.0pt\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathrm{}}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.63959){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\smash{\begin{tabular}[t]{l}\noindent\hskip 42.67912pt\scalebox{8}{$\displaystyle\sim$}\end{tabular}}}}\put(0.77218,0.10885){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip-17.07182pt\par\noindent\hskip 8.5359pt\scalebox{5}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathrm{}i}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateM.pdf}}\end{picture}}\end{matrix}\penalty\ \penalty\ =

\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \left\{\frac{1}{\#\left[\tilde{\breve{\mathcal{C}}}_{{\mathrm{A}};{\mathrm{}m,j}}\right]}:\begin{matrix}\scalebox{0.1}{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateM.pdf}}\put(0.40119,0.42637){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[335.53992pt]\raggedright\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.60885){\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip 65.0pt-\heightof{\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\breve{\mathcal{C}}}_{{\mathrm{A}}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}}/2\hskip 72.0pt\scalebox{8}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\breve{\mathcal{C}}}_{{\mathrm{A}}}^{\phantom{\dagger}}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.27218,0.63959){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\smash{\begin{tabular}[t]{l}\noindent\hskip 42.67912pt\scalebox{8}{$\displaystyle\sim$}\end{tabular}}}}\put(0.77218,0.10885){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0)[lt]{\begin{minipage}[391.46324pt]\raggedright\vskip-17.07182pt\par\noindent\hskip 8.5359pt\scalebox{5}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\displaystyle{\mathrm{}m,j}$}}\@add@raggedright\end{minipage}}}\put(0.0,0.0){\includegraphics[width,page]{gateM.pdf}}\end{picture}}\end{matrix}\,\right\}.

(C.334)

The implementation of IIAM is similar to the standard procedure (see App.
C.2.2
), the only difference is that
\tilde{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathrm{}};{\mathrm{}i}}
is now a stochastic mixture of circuits. So each time we should sample
\tilde{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathrm{}};{\mathrm{}i}}
we choose one of the constituent circuits of
\tilde{\breve{\mathcal{C}}}_{{\mathrm{A}};{\mathrm{}m,j}}
uniformly at random and then divide the output by the multiplicity (
\#\left[\tilde{\breve{\mathcal{C}}}_{{\mathrm{A}};{\mathrm{}m,j}}\right]
).

The execution time for every circuit contained within
\tilde{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathrm{A}};{\mathrm{}m}}
is:

\displaystyle\tau_{\mathrm{E},j}=

\displaystyle\left(1+\frac{2m}{N_{\mathrm{G}}}\right)\tau_{\mathrm{E}}

(C.335)

where
\tau_{\mathrm{E}}
is the execution time for the original noisy circuit and we have assumed that all gates take the same amount of time to implement.

Numerical and analytical calculations have indicated that the proxy bias is given by:

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle\heartsuit}};{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle M}}}=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\frac{\left(2N_{\mathrm{G}}p\right)^{N_{\mathrm{M}}+1}}{\left(N_{\mathrm{M}}+1\right)!}&\text{ for stochastic noise}\\
\frac{\left(N_{\mathrm{G}}\left|\phi\right|\right)^{N_{\mathrm{M}}+1}}{\left(N_{\mathrm{M}}+1\right)!}&\text{ for rotational errors}\\
\end{cases}

(C.336)

to leading order.

It is worth noting that Table
6
is slightly different from the corresponding table in Ref.
[
34
]
(see Table I of Ref.
[
34
]
or Table II of the Arxiv version: Ref.
[
44
]
). The reason for this difference is that there is a degree of freedom in the choice of coefficients when
N_{\mathrm{M}}=4
and one wishes to mitigate local depolarising noise. We choose coefficients that will also mitigate rotational errors and the authors of Ref.
[
34
]
make a different choice. In addition it appears there may be a typographic error for the coefficient:
a_{\left\{3,3\right\}}
in the fourth order mitigation (corresponding to
c_{{\mathrm{A},2};{\mathrm{}1}}
in this work). We obtain consistent results using:

C.9
IILM

Identity-insertion-assisted local mitigation (IILM) is a local method (see App.
C.2.1
) that uses identity insertions to amplify the noise in the noisy gate variants:
